ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e3123	Ladies and gentlemen! First, I would like to say that the employment rate or the employment rate in Austria, especially last year, including under this Federal Government, has developed extremely positively, that we have rising employment and falling unemployment. In particular, women's employment has increased significantly last year. There has also been a dramatic reduction in the number of women's unemployment in Austria in general. This is a very positive development that we must continue to support in a sustainable way so that this trend continues accordingly. That is to say, we have a situation on the Austrian labour market today, which, as a result of which we can happily appreciate compared to other European countries, is that we are moving towards full employment. And this is also the goal that this Federal Government is sustaining with all its measures on the labour market. As far as the female employment rate in relation to childcare benefits is concerned, I have already told you that the essential thing about this childcare allowance is the fact that women have the option – and every woman individually for themselves – to decide whether to stay at home with their child in these first three years or whether they want to be employed while ensuring professional childcare at the same time. I assume that this has significantly improved the compatibility of work and family for women, especially because this child care allowance provides a maximum of 200 000 s per year. If They bear in mind that, in the past, only a commitment of around 4,000 s was possible, it is clear that this measure actually drove women away from the labour market. Thus, contrary to the argument of the Socialist Group, what we are now doing is a measure to enable women to work even during the period of child care, thereby improving their situation on the labour market, their position on the labour market, their commitment to the operation accordingly. I therefore assume that child benefits will only have a positive impact on the employment of women and, I would like to stress, will have positive effects in terms of childcare facilities. What has been said many times over the past few weeks is completely wrong, namely that there will be no or fewer childcare places in Austria. On the contrary: We also achieve that day mothers, baby cribs and similar facilities for children between one and three years of age can also be funded accordingly.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e3163	Next question: Mrs. Dr. Baumgartner-Gabitzer. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e3178	Dear Mrs. Vice chancellor! My question is: [[/PAKT/VG/XXI/M/M_00075/index.shtml -- 75/M]] What has been the results of the Commission on Task Reform set up by you so far?
ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e3205	Please answer, Mrs. Vice chancellor.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e3221	Ladies and gentlemen! The Task Reform Commission under the direction of Professor Raschauer has held a number of meetings in recent months and will present its final report at the end of this week. The main objective of this Commission for Task Reform was to ask the question – perhaps to say it a little provocatively –: What do we need the state at all, that is, what tasks does the state have to perform, and what tasks can be done better, cheaper, faster and more efficiently elsewhere? There are a number of competences to question both the federal government and the countries, cities and communities, because it must be the top objective of any administrative reform to carry out a task reform in the sense that both tasks and complex dual or multiple competences can be left out at all. The objective of the Task Reform Commission was and will be – and this will be so read in the final report – to ensure appropriate administrative simplification for citizens. These include, for example, the reduction in the administrative procedures between the Federal Government and the Länder, or a clear simplification of the commercial procedure, and thereby a relief of small and medium-sized enterprises in that country. Bureaucratic reduction is primarily something that should benefit the public, not the state. It is our intention, therefore, that in the future there should only be a public contact point – that is the district administration authority, the district headquarters in the ideal case – so that the government marathon will stop for the citizen and the situation in Austria will look such that the citizens will no longer be running, but the files!

ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e3681	Please answer, Mrs. Vice chancellor.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e3696	I believe that the new national law on teaching services is a crucial and essential step forward in terms of working time regulations in the public service. This model is cost-neutral, which is why the finding you made in the past, namely that there will be a loss of salary, is not understandable to me. The purpose of this national teacher's working time model is that there should be an annual working time, that one should no longer, as before, just depend on the pure teaching time, but that one takes into account that there are hours in which the teacher stands in the class and teaches that there are pre- and post-preparation periods and that there are also other activities of the teacher such as Ordinaries, Custodiates and the like. It is therefore necessary to take into account the total annual working time of a teacher, but also to document it. It must be understandable what the teacher does in teaching time. You know that we have developed this model together with the trade union and in agreement with the trade union, which is also proof to me that, in the case of constructive willingness to discuss interests, good solutions from trade unions are possible. I would also like to express my express thanks to the compulsory teachers' union. I would also like to see the same willingness to cooperate with other trade unions, and we would continue to do so. I think it's a good model. Annual working time models are something we strive for for the entire public service. We are also working on this in the individual departments, because there are specific conditions which vary depending on the division. But annual working time models are the future for working time calculations in the public service.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e3727	Additional question? - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e3742	Mrs. Federal Minister! ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e3760	Please answer, Mrs. Vice chancellor.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e3776	You know my point of view. I believe, in principle, that we suffer from different systems not only between the private sector and the public service, but also within the public service. We have a Pragmatisation system-old, a Pragmatization system-new, a contract staff law-old, a new contract-service service-new – which leads to very different pay curves within the staff of the public service and is also not motivating for performance. For me, the highest premise of a remuneration reform in the public service is a performance-oriented reward, which is also the prerequisite for the implementation of New Public Management. Otherwise, you have no incentive for public service employees – especially young employees – and this applies not only to teachers, but also to all other employees in the public service – to also provide the best possible services. That is, we are working on developing a salary system that is performance-oriented, which no longer depends on age, which no longer depends on seniority, but on the performance and qualification of the individual employee, on the classification of the function he has, and no longer on the automatic age rule. Switzerland has already developed a model in this area – and implemented at both canton level and federal level – which I consider to be exemplary, also for Austria.

We have seen in Austria – perhaps excluding the winter sports sector – that there is often a problem, because we are promoting talent in competence centres, but we are unable to allow these young people to move into top-level sports or further development into top-level sports. We have set up an expert working group to pursue this objective within the framework of the Top Sport Austria project.

From this point of view, the question of financial compensatory negotiations between the Federal Government and countries is also to be seen. What I have said and what I have said is that I do not think much of the fact that from the outset we are already making decisions on what we are allowed to talk about and what we cannot talk about, but it must be possible to find the question of the objectively best solution of responsibilities. In Austria, we suffer very much from the fact that we have a wealth of materials in which there are dual and multi-competences, which are not understandable to the public, which allow the procedures to last too long and which therefore also lead to high costs for the individual citizen. There is an urgent need to remedy this situation.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e4854	Dear Mrs. Vice-Chancellor! What role does the administrative reform generally have in improving the proximity of citizens to you?
ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e4869	Mrs. Vice chancellor, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e4885	Closeness to the citizen must be the main objective of any administrative reform, because I believe that we are living today in a situation where administrative processes, legislative processes for citizens have become so complicated and impermeable that the democratic possibility of participation of the citizen is reduced accordingly. I think we must confess that close proximity must be top priority. This is not only true of the national level, but also of the European level. Although the Maastricht Treaty contains the word'subsidiarity', it is still not a matter of life. The Europe of the regions has largely disappeared from the discussion. We must return to saying: Citizens' concerns are as close as possible to the citizen as possible and can therefore be done as quickly and as easily as possible. This also means that there must be performance targets for the administration, which must also be met, such as models that have already been implemented very successfully in other countries, which say that certain applications must be made within a certain period of time; otherwise, if no decision is taken by the Authority, these concerns are deemed approved, for example in the right of plant authorisation. These are measures that simplify the administration accordingly and give the citizen the certainty that he does not have to wait until the Sankt-Nimmerleins day until there is a decision in his case. This is the primary concern of any administrative reform.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e7326	Mr President. Mrs. Vice chancellor! Ladies and gentlemen! The 1999 sports report, as has been said by many, is the first legible work. He was praised on the scope and transparency of all the committees, both in the Committee and here in plenary. The former mystery and camouflage of financial grants is finally over. There is nothing to hide or hide. Financial support is open and is an important part of the reporting system. However, due to the time of discussion on this 1999 sports report, the actuality is no longer to the extent necessary, so that today, as a sports speaker of my party, I would like to make some fundamental considerations about sports for the first time. Sports is an essential part of a living society and is a high value for many people in their personal development. In the eyes of most people, he stands for excellence. However, the average person only acts as a consumer from the media or at sports events as a live spectator. Top performances motivate many of us to engage in sports activities, they are stimulating and also create national and regional identification. However, pride on the achievements of our athletes should not discriminate against those of other nations and continents. A emotional "we" is a positive part of our range of values, and a healthy local rivality – such as in ice hockey between Klagenfurt and Villach or in football between Rapid and Austria Vienna – leads to exciting games and, of course, to better performances. Because of the fact that many sports top performances appear to be unattainable, they are not often seen as depressing. That is why I would like to refer in particular to the importance of broadband and the benefits of the meaningful movement. Movement – as far as possible in the wild – is the basis for vitality, joy of life and performance. It is an essential component of a life in health regardless of age. The foundation stone for sporty inclinations and motor skills is laid in the family, in kindergartens, in schools, as well as in our sports clubs. This is why it is primarily important to devote our attention to these areas. An effective family promotion includes not only financial grants, but also close and manageable sports venues, for example. We cannot therefore abandon all ski resorts or indoor pools simply because they require public grants. The closure of sports facilities often also means the loss of leisure care, less animo to the movement and thus a worse health of the population, which is also associated, for example, with an increase in hospital costs. In practice, the proximity to a sports venue is not often determined whether and especially how often we are able to engage in sports. Therefore, consideration should be given to the location of sports facilities and fitness facilities in the construction of residential settlements, especially in the urban area. I think and suggest that it would be useful, for example, to devote part of the housing aid to health and sport so that more can be done in the housing areas in this direction. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] It goes without saying that especially in these sports venues, attention must be paid to disabled people in Austria. In school sports, for example, we need, in order to list just a few keywords, a better training of teachers, especially in public schools, in addition to professionally trained experts who can be used across schools, an increased integration of the sports associations and, of course, suitable premises. The same applies to the workplace, and this issue is becoming increasingly important. The companies have long recognized that incentives in the sports sector lead to an increase in the performance of employees, which, of course, also results in higher productivity. Increased willingness to do so brings greater competitiveness to our businesses, but also, of course, to our economy as a whole. Tax incentives for undertakings should be provided in this respect. I am thinking, for example, of the use of unemployed teachers who, in cooperation with sports experts, could work out and implement projects together with the companies. The acquisition of team ability, the creation of common goals, the enjoyment of victories, but also, for example, the processing of defeats in the sports clubs makes our youth, but not only these, viable and relative. Therefore, in the year of voluntaryity, I propose a special award for Austrian sports officials and I would like to thank all the employees in the umbrella organisations – the Austrian gymnastics and sports union, the ASKÖ and the ASVÖ – as well as in all professional associations for their valuable activity in the service of the general public. [[It is a surprise to the ÖVP, the Freeman and the Deputies of the SPÖ.]] Sports clubs are an important part of the civil society. Thousands of officials work in 12 200 clubs and volunteerly care for 2.5 million members. This work represents an equivalent of about 8.5 billion schilling per year – if you count only 100 s per hour, it results in the number of 85 million hours done volunteerly in this field. In the past, the traditional sports associations have done a great deal of work, are constantly developing and offer our people the highest level of sports. To promote and support all of them is therefore our greatest concern in the field of sports policy. Those who perform sensible leisure activities within the framework of a well-run sports club are motivated by pure consumer behaviour to action, not only once, but regularly, in the community of a sports club and cared for by well-trained professionals. However, young people increasingly prefer public places to operate so-called trends. This is not about willingness to compete or competition, but very often about the fun to have fun and entertainment. With this in mind, I encourage the targeted and emotional use of street workers, so that these young people can be properly cared for without restricting the freedom of these young people. Ladies and gentlemen! Overall, sport and movement lead to a life-loving, powerful and vital population. The goal of bringing movement to our country must motivate us to pursue an offensive sports policy. All are invited to participate in the sports Austria. – Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e7922	Mr President. Mrs. Vice chancellor! High house! I now turn to the 1999 sports report again. The 1999 sports year will remain in positive memory. The Nordic World Ski Championships in the Ramsau am Dachstein were both organizational and sportingly a full success. I would like to congratulate all Nordic athletes on their great achievements. On this occasion I would also like to congratulate the summer sportsmen on their excellent sporting results and thank all sportsmen, officials and officials for the work done last year. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] With the formation of the new government in February 2000, the sportsmen came to the responsibility of Mrs. Vice-Chancellor, thus giving Ms. Dr. Riess-Passer the opportunity – as she pointed out in her foreword – to take up her first professional experience as an employee in the Austrian Ski Association and to further develop in a responsible political position. As sport develops personality, integrates and creates identification, according to Dr. Riess-Passer, the promotion of sport a health, social, economic and employment-related task. Mrs. Vice-Chancellor is committed to this task. I believe that we should demonstrate in sport how to achieve ambitious goals together, and I am convinced that at the end of this period of government we can look back on successful sports policy reorientation in our country, together with the federal sports organisations, the umbrellas and trade associations and also the Olympic Committee, the 12 000 sports clubs and hundreds of thousands of athletes. Now for sport promotion in detail: In 1999 a total of 707 million Schilling were spent on sports promotion. The general sport promotion amounted to 440 million schillings, which were distributed as follows: Around 95 million Schilling went to the Bundessportsorganisation, 148 million Schilling to the ÖFB, 60.4 million Schilling each to the three major sports roof associations ASKÖ, ASVÖ and the Association as well as 14.6 million Schilling to the OOC. The sports report takes account of what these funds have been allocated or spent on, but also refers to separate sports promotions by the countries. Another chapter in the 1999 sports report focuses on training and youth work. In the framework of the project "VerbandförderungEngwuchs" numerous sports associations received funds to set up a targeted young people's promotion. Schools with sports priorities and various training training courses were also funded. Many students received sports scholarships and were funded by one of the many sports institutions, including the "sports aid". Such sport support is also provided by the countries and individual resorts, such as the Federal Ministry of State Defense within the framework of the HSNS. As I have said many times, the HSNS is a very, very important catch-up network for athletes who practice sports that are not so public. A separate section of the report is devoted to women and sport. It is primarily about taking greater account of the particular circumstances of women's sports, supporting active athletes more effectively, and also increasing the proportion of women in the sports bodies gradually. [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] In this respect, an own workshop was organised in the year under review and a theme-oriented working group was set up. Some chapters of this report deal with the fight against doping, sports medicine and the Austrian Olympic and Sports Museum. An extensive annex, consisting of the sports legislation section in Austria, together with the relevant national legislation, a member statistics of the individual sports organisations and a detailed list of addresses and an organigram, completes the informative and comprehensive report. Disabled sport faces major challenges – according to the title on page 197 of the current report. Disability sports are facing great challenges because, on the one hand, the number of people with disabilities who want to participate in the sport is constantly increasing, and on the other hand, the circle of those people who fall under the term "barriers" is being expanded. There are, on the one hand, the classic disabled associations in which the amputated, the blind, the visually impaired, the deaf, the hearing impaired, the mentally disabled and the disabled with paralysis are active, and a second group of disabled persons, such as people after organ transplants, infarction-damaged, in other words, persons where the disability cannot be recognized at first sight. It is quite understandable that the Austrian Handicrafts' Association feels super vista, in a first statement, that the second group is also involved in its field, especially in the light of the fact that the existing financial, human and organizational resources are not enough at present. It will therefore be all our task to assist disabled sport with a bundle of measures. First of all, local sports clubs must open up more than ever to the sport of disabled people. It will be the task of the countries and municipalities to achieve steering effects in the framework of their subsidy policy for these sports associations. Secondly, it will be necessary to improve the training of disabled sports associations. This will also require the appropriate training of the teacher in the gym. Thirdly, it will be necessary to ensure medium and long-term financial resources for the Austrian Disabled Sport Association in order to be able to offer the corresponding offers to all groups of disabled people. Ladies and gentlemen! However, it is not only about broadband in the disabled, but it will also be necessary to promote top-level sport in the field of the disabled. An essential signal in the top-level sport of the disabled is a sign for all disabled people to participate more than ever actively in the sport. I would like to thank Mrs Vice-Chancellor for having given a clear and clear response to the issue of top-level sport in the disabled. Among other things, it states that financing the top-level sport of disabled people is an important priority of the Government Convention. I would also like to thank Mrs. Vice-Chancellor for her declared goal of providing the disabled athletes with the great achievements of giving them the recognition they deserve. And I would also like to thank Mrs Vice-Chancellor for having achieved that the promotion of Austrian disabled sport was not subject to any budget commitment and was further increased in the framework of general federal sport funding for 2002. Thank you very much, Mrs. Vice chancellor! [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] I therefore call on the media, particularly the ORF and, in particular, the printing media, to give greater importance to the sport of disabled people in their reporting. For example, at the vocational school for sanitary, heating and air conditioning technology in Vienna's Mollardgasse, only five advisory hours per week are available for 960 students. If we were to expect all of this to be socially political, we would have to find a large majority in favour of it if They would make a proposal to extend consultation opportunities and also to secure the necessary money. In my opinion, social prevention is always cheaper than social healing, ladies and gentlemen. For today we, the Social Democrats in this House, are satisfied that the resolution of the National Council ensures the continuation of student guidance at vocational schools. All political groups will vote in favour of this resolution. For tomorrow, however, we need a possibility of extending the consultation period on a clear, legal basis, just as the advisory teachers demand with their citizens' initiative and, as I believe, rightly demand. The help at the right time is simply more humane and better than the treatment of developments and damage afterwards with more effort and – I said so – also with higher social costs. My group therefore agrees with the resolution. It is an important step in the right direction – but further steps must follow in the interest of our youth. Ladies and gentlemen! Finally, on behalf of our group, I would like to express my warm thanks to all the consultants who have turned to their youthful guardians, the apprentices, in many hours which have not been paid, from this point of view.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e12379	Mr President. Federal Minister! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! Mrs Steibl! I think you should take this comment back. It has already been mentioned: The 1999 Family Report is available. I want to deal with poverty in the families. Family policy is currently marked by a major contradiction in Austria: On the one hand, comparative scientific studies show that expenditure on families is in absolute peak, and the planned introduction of childcare allowance from the beginning of 2002 will further strengthen this. At the same time, the birth rate continues to decline. On the other hand, families of poverty are particularly vulnerable or many of them have to live in poverty, especially some types of families. This applies, among other things, to households of single earners in low-paid industries, to households of single parents, to households of long-term unemployed, to households of migrants and so on – I could continue this list, but that would be my time frame. As a result, the poverty of families in Austria is determined by three factors. Firstly, the amount of household income. To stress this seems particularly important to me, because while children reduce per capita income in a household, a household has enough income, i.e. a high income, so even three and more children do not pose a risk of poverty. Secondly, The employment of both parents reduces the risk of poverty of families. This is also evidenced by statistics. On the one hand, single parents and their children are particularly often confronted with the negative effects of poverty, and at the same time households with several children are particularly vulnerable when only one parent is employed. Therefore, the compatibility of family and employment is a particularly important social requirement for SPÖ. Thirdly, Poverty is different. As I have already pointed out, not all families are affected to the same extent of poverty, but poverty affects in particular some groups. For families, the risk of poverty will inevitably become poverty if several risks come together. This applies, for example, if a parent loses his job or becomes a single earner inability to work. The SPÖ therefore pursues a focus in family policy, which is clearly different from that of the government. We must focus on concrete measures to reduce the risk of poverty for families. The risk of poverty in families can only be reduced if measures are taken to enable both parents to pursue employment. This means that measures are needed to reconcile work and family life in the working world through family-friendly standards in labour law, such as sufficient part-time jobs and new working time models. [[Congratulation with the SPÖ]] Nor can it be that the flexibilization of the working world only serves the economy. For parents, the right to shortened working hours must be legitimised. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] At the same time, however, counter-strategies are also needed against the ever-increasing deviation from normal working hours. Despite the demand for flexible shop opening times, for example, families need to secure common leisure blocks. Another area where SPÖ calls for significant improvements is the re-entry of women after the retirement period. Specific measures for this would, for example, be different contact-keeping strategies and forced parental education and advice. So what the SPÖ calls for is measures to improve the compatibility of family and work. Both must be treated as equal social areas. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] In addition, however, there is a need to create a second framework for sustainable ways out of the risk of poverty. In this respect, an increase in family benefits alone, as planned with the childcare allowance, is little targeting. Instead, institutional support for families – for women and men need to be made easier to assume responsibility for a child – through the expansion of public health and services and the development of public childcare facilities. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] ==References====External links== In May 2000 the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection discussed or, more generally, not dealt with, because at the time the opposition parties preferred to hold a debate on the Rules of Procedure instead of discussing the problems of the family – which was ultimately a reason for the late adoption of this report. ==References== In October last year, there was the expert hearing on the individual chapters of the report, and I quote some of the experts' statements: "The share of expenditure for families is embarrassingly small." - An expert statement. Secondly, "The consequences and risks of a parenthood are still higher for women than for men, especially with a view to subsequent separation." And the third statement of an expert: "Households with children and, above all, multi-child families are still economically disadvantaged." So there is a great need for action in family policy. Dr. Mertel: Have you ever looked in there?]] [[Abg. Dr. Mertel: Have you ever done that before?]] The most important but worrying phenomenon has already been mentioned today, which is the reduction of the birth rate to a number that is now 1.34. It can be seen from this report that the number of births has almost halved over the last 30 years; in 1970 it was still 2.29. The last figure is from 1998, but we do not know how far it has fallen in the last two years. However, as the report points out, the family is also subject to very substantial changes and major changes in many other areas. There are fewer family formations, the marriage usually takes place later – this time has shifted by five years – resulting in a later motherhood, there are fewer children per family – only over 8.5 percent of the families have more than two children – unfortunately also more divorces – four out of ten marriages will be divorced again – and what is also socially negative is the fact that there are fewer and fewer multigenerational families. At the end also a not very nice number: There are no more children living in three quarters of all households. Someone who has children in the house and knows how much joy you can have with children also knows that this is certainly not a positive development. Ladies and gentlemen! Many families, especially multi-child families and single parents, live on the edge of or in poverty. The billing of family benefits is therefore a command of the hour. The rapid introduction of the childcare allowance, largely in line with the free movement model, is therefore an important decision taken by the Federal Government. [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Finally, I would like to say a sentence that I have heard from an expert and that I really like very well: In future, we should ensure that policy decisions are always examined for family compatibility. – I think this is very, very important. Families are the foundation for the next generation. If the foundation is good, then the opportunity for the next generation is good. The Federal Government has made a significant contribution to strengthening this foundation with the childcare allowance.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e13659	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! I would like to take the lead in understanding the general legal situation: The Court of First Instance now has to limit its investigation to the extent specified by the public prosecutor's application. In addition, it shall not be empowered to review this application for its validity. Except in the case of illegality, it is also not entitled to refuse the implementation of pre-examination steps. It is therefore also for the public prosecutor to decide which file material is to be transmitted to the examiner for the performance of the survey steps he has requested. In the present proceedings, in which by the Public Prosecutor's Office Vienna had already completed some of the facts, and now the survey results of the economic police had been completed. According to their view, for the Vienna Procuratorate, the case regarding some suspects was considered to be final due, without the need for judicial preliminary surveys. In accordance with the provisions of the Public Prosecutor's Act, it has therefore submitted a project report to the Upper State Prosecutor's Office in Vienna and has attached the relevant survey results submitted by the Economic Police. These documents were submitted to the Upper State Prosecutor's Office in Vienna and have now been sent to the competent department of the Federal Ministry of Justice. In view of further facts, concrete preliminary investigations are required by the public prosecutor's office in Vienna. Therefore, in mid-March 2001, the Court of Justice of the State Criminal Matters of Vienna made the necessary requests, in particular concerning the responsible hearing of six suspects and the investigation and investigation by the economic police of some witnesses. In order to make available to the examiner all the necessary results necessary for the implementation of these preliminary surveys, the necessary information was produced on the occasion of the separation of the file, if not already available, and the survey results were divided by facts. The documents required for this purpose have been made available to the examiner. The final report drawn up by the economic police has not been sent by the public prosecutor Vienna to the examiner for reasons of clarity, because it also contains in-depth statements on facts relating to both the proceedings which have already been initiated and on such facts, which have been dealt with in the field of the Special Commission, but are not the subject of the proceedings pending at the public prosecutor's office in Vienna. The public prosecutor’s office in Vienna pointed out that the procedure chosen prior to its application had also been agreed with the examiner in the context of an in-depth personal debate, and it was also agreed that documents still considered necessary can be submitted immediately. After the Court of First Instance had expressed its doubts about the application and, in particular, the form of file transmission by the Vienna Public Prosecutor's Office in a multi-page file note, the following procedure was subsequently adopted in the interest of a successful cooperation between the public prosecutor's office in Vienna and the competent examiner: The Vienna Public Prosecutor's Office requests the Economic Police to re-transmit billing of the results of the survey submitted to the Upper State Prosecutor's Office in Vienna and to draw up a summary of the facts still open to the public prosecutor's office in Vienna. These documents are then sent to the Court of Justice of the State for Criminal Matters Vienna, together with the criminal act of the public prosecutor's office in Vienna, to be transmitted by him. In the meantime, the examiner has received these documents. ==References====External links== The final report, drawn up on 3 March 2001, also submitted the original provisional final report for consideration. ==References====External links==* Official website In the case of partial investigation orders by the public prosecutor, which were filed in March 2001, it was not entitled to leave the entire file beyond the level of the investigation and was not entitled to a misunderstanding of the judicial role in the criminal proceedings. These appointments must be made in an indefinite manner, as is the case with all the designations of judges. This gives rise to the possibility that more judges can be appointed to a court than judges are systematic. In the event of the return of a judge from the leave of a karenz, a so-called victory arose. If another case occurs in another court, for example through karenz leave, the law provides that the staff Senate of the Supreme Court, i.e. a judicial body which cannot be given instructions, assigns to that court the judge who is the dominant. Such temporary allocation shall in principle take place within the same court proceedings, in rare exceptional cases within the Oberlandsgerichtsprengegel, but shall never take place outside the Oberlandsgerichtsprengegel. The Federal Ministry of Justice has no influence on these allocations. If Dr. Erdi said in an interview that he could even be transferred to Carinthia, he would be subject to a double error of law. [[Abg. Dr. Fekter: Wrong! That's deliberately wrong!]] On the one hand, this is not a change, on the other hand, the law excludes allocations outside the Supreme Court. This is an absolutely necessary legal tool for staff Senates to ensure, in the event of cases of maternity protection, that judges are actually used in the courts in sufficient number. These allocations must be made in accordance with precise legal requirements. As the Vice-President of the Upper Regional Court of Vienna, Dr. Schläffer, who is a member of the Staff Senate, has, and is not foreseen, such an assignment by Dr. Erdi. Arbitrations of judges are excluded in Austria due to the constitutional and simple legal situation. I would like to stress that judicial independence is a fundamental value of our democratic rule of law on every occasion. Our common understanding must be that ensuring judicial independence is a task for all of us as operators of our state and is an essential part of our responsibility. The number of childless couples is increasing, there are fewer and more later births. There has also been a decline in marriages and a simultaneous increase in divorces. The average number of children per woman is 1.34 in Austria today. This is the lowest value ever recorded in Austria. In Northern Europe it is much higher, in the south of Europe, in Italy and Spain, slightly lower. There has been little change in the division of family work in recent years. For the most part, family work is still carried out by the female partner. Although the role and self-understanding of men is changing, the major contribution to the recovery and care of family members or to assistance for them is still being made by women. On the other hand, this increase also fits in with the logic of the Austrian Criminal Code. With us, it is always always when someone commits a criminal act and there is a dead person in it, without he insisting on it, usually even without even wanting to have it in the worst way, so that the threat of punishment is immediately raised from 20 years to "life-long", for example, in the arson, in the robbery, in the risk of nuclear energy, in the risk of explosives, in the intentional risk, in the unauthorized handling of nuclear material. – All these are offences, which are quite different from the killing of a human being or of several people. It's about property crimes like robbery, it's about everything possible when it comes to arson. But at least you don't want someone to die, you don't count on it either. Nevertheless, when it happens, the threat of punishment is "life-long". It's much more dramatic when it comes to drug trafficking in a big way. There the person there expects him to throw many, many, mostly very young, ever younger people into misery and transport many of them into early death. That means he's agreeing to what's happening. And following the logic that is inherent in our criminal law and which I have tried to present to you, it is also clear that in such a case "life-long" must be the threat, otherwise these internationally active corporate lords in the poison field will be treated better than a robber, who – for which reasons always – will strike once too firmly with the consequence that someone remains without wanting to justify it, or a brand who wants to commit a property crime, and then remains a dead person on the track. That means that it is not a group of recipients who deserve our pity only in the worst possible way. I don't know why you're breaking your head about whether you can't get any evidence problems into your mind. This is always the case in criminal proceedings, because if one condemns an innocent person for 18 years in criminal proceedings, that is not exactly funny for the system. Whoever can't be proved that he's been doing something, he's supposed to get no less punishment, but he's acquitted. It fits into the European environment – these are ahead of us for nostrils in this respect – and it fits into the system of Austrian criminal justice. From our point of view, the Right of Warranty Amendment Act is the most important consumer protection law of the last ten years. I regret that the importance of civil law provisions is underestimated in the public debate. It is underestimated in terms of criminal law, reform policy, but the civil law field, which affects every individual, particularly in the area of shortcomings. There are problems in the construction, crafts, car purchase, computer purchase, software purchase and so on. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! In our view, these regulations are of particular importance for legal certainty in economic matters, both for consumers and for the economy. This right-of-guarantee amendment law implements the so-called "Consumer Goods Directive", and I emphasize: It is an advantage that comes from the European Union to Austria, which gives us the opportunity to make adjustments to the old Austrian warranty law. The government, however, and I would like to say this with all clarity, is limited to implementing only this directive and does not create any further measures, Mr Federal Minister. Anders Deutschland: A modernisation of the law on debt has been launched in the context of a general reform of the law on debt. ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e26791	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! Whoever followed the news more closely yesterday evening has seen the problems of German politics with the castor transports mentioned by my predecessor. [[Abg. May. Lunacek: I mentioned the atomic excretion scenario!]] Both the old and the new government of the Federal Republic have had to use police forces against demonstrators, and the government, as we saw yesterday, has also had Greenpeace activists removed there. The place Dannenberg has equaled a place in the state of emergency. Ladies and gentlemen! In Austria, we are not fortunately confronted with such events. As a result, it was all the more gratifying that we in the Committee on Foreign Affairs had a high level of agreement with the two government proposals, both in the Joint Convention on the Safety of the treatment of spent fuel and on the safety of the treatment of radioactive waste as well as in the second government template, the Additional Protocol to the Convention on the Non-Proliferation of Nuclear Weapons. [[Abg. Murauer:... the Greens are not in government! Then it'll be better.]] The objective of the Joint Convention is to achieve the highest possible safety standards for the treatment of spent fuel and radioactive waste by linking national and international measures worldwide. Risks caused by ionising radiation are to be reduced by preventing radiological accidents in this way. Ladies and gentlemen! For the first time, this Agreement establishes obligations under international law in order to apply internationally recognised principles of radiation protection in the field of waste treatment also in national, i.e. national, national, national, national, and national. In the agreed international review and in international control lies compared to the previous legal situation of progress. In addition, the contract also regulates the mutual information obligations among the contracting parties. It also improves the participation rights of neighbouring countries in the waste convention. The second government template, the Additional Protocol on the Non-Proliferation of Nuclear Weapons, aims to prevent the misuse of civilian nuclear products for military purposes from being used even more effectively than before. You know the history. In 1972, under the Nuclear Non-Proliferation Treaty with the International Atomic Energy Agency, Austria concluded an agreement on nuclear safety controls. After accession to the EU, this Treaty was then replaced by the so-called EURATOM Agreement. After the Gulf War, however, it was then necessary to recognize that a contractual partner did not comply with the block agreement, but was in the sense of secretly developing a nuclear weapons programme that had not been discovered despite IAEA's controls. In order to remedy such system failures, a concept has been drawn up since 1993 to improve security controls. Two important demands have been included in this Additional Protocol, namely the obligation to provide information to the Contracting States and, as I have already said, I am already at the end of the procedure, an improved possibility for control of the inspectors. [[President Dr. Fischer gives the bell sign]] I would ask you to agree to both agreements.

If this law is not passed now, I think we must consider very carefully what form better support can still be achieved at international level.

They meant that Canada already has a high balance sheet on the implementation of human rights. On this point I quite agree with you. But even in countries with a very good human rights situation, there can be things that may not yet be quite as good! [[Intervocations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] [[Unrest in the hall.]] Mr President. In view of the high noise level, I would like to say: I would be very pleased if the ladies and gentlemen of the government could listen at such a later hour, or if they did not listen to the plan, leave the hall! [[Intrusion of the Abg. May. Mainoni.]] If I do not even listen to the President of the Council, I will try to speak, but perhaps at least Mrs Hakl will hear it! [[Congratulation with the greens.]] If a country such as Canada, even if it has a good human rights record, has not yet done everything it has decided on its own – in the case of the implementation of a collective property right recognised by the Canadian Supreme Court in 1997 – then it must be possible among friends to ask and say: Please do it! – I do not see any interference in it, and I think it is very regrettable that Austria says only: They're gonna do this! I mean, among friends, it would be possible to also ask Canada to implement this! On the ratification or non-ratification of the ILO Convention 169: Mrs Jäger has already mentioned that this non-ration is now a very bizarre matter in the history of the Austrian National Council of the last eight years. Those of you already in the XVIII. ==References====External links==* Official website In June 1993, the government decided unanimously that it should be called upon to ratify this Convention. But a call was apparently not enough. In the XX. In 1997, the National Council unanimously decided that the government should be ratified. What happened? – Nothing has happened! Time has passed. At that time, the reason for the non-ratification was that one meant by the Social Ministry: We have no indigenouss in Austria, why should we decide? At that time, the Secretary of State was still the Secretary of State, and at that time she was very likely for ratification. The real reason for this is that Mrs. Jäger, too, mentioned in part, is that it is mainly about protecting the habitats of indigenous peoples in areas where Austria is implementing development projects or companies. And not even the fact that 160 Austrian climate alliance communities – unfortunately I have not come to see if perhaps some mayors from these communities are sitting here – have asked the government to sign this convention could change something. And now the information comes from the foreign office, from the Federal Minister, that we cannot sign it because this could disturb coherence in Austrian national law. I have already reminded you in the Committee that in the ILO Convention 169 it is quite clear that a better legal situation in the respective country is of course not affected by the ratification of this Convention. This argument is simply not in order, and it covers arguments that, as I have heard, go in the direction: We cannot allow that, because then perhaps the Roma will also come and say that they want similar rights as they are formulated in the Convention! Or maybe even the Carinthian Slovenians could come and say that they were there first and not the German-speaking people and that for this reason they want to have more rights! In addition, it might become more difficult for Austrian companies to participate in international large-scale projects, in which, for example, people were expelled from their territory in the past in the case of dam projects. – These are the reasons why you will not agree this time and Austria, which has often expressed itself positively in the past and has also done some to protect indigenous peoples, does not want to ratify it now. Instead, there is only one motion for a resolution in the Committee, in which it says something blurring that although we do not do the ratification, we will do everything we can to ensure that the Convention is implemented. Mrs. Minister! I am very surprised about this, and above all about the argument that only 14 other countries have agreed to it. This attitude, as Austria has towards other countries, is very unfortunate for me: We're always just doing what the others have done. We're not saying: We sign this because we think it makes sense. No, no, no, Rather, we say: The others don't do that, then we'd rather not do it, because otherwise something could happen to us! Mrs. Minister! Unfortunately, this attitude as a foreign policy attitude of the Austrian Federal Government is very counterproductive!
ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e27290	Next speaker is Deputy Magistrate. Posch. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-03-28-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00062_d7e27311	Mrs. Minister! Mr President. High house! Unfortunately, they will reject the request concerning the indigenous population in Canada. That is to be accepted, but it is to be regretful! Without wanting to address the tragic history of European land takeover in the New Continent, it must be noted that Indians still belong to 50 percent of the state area and that they have never been abandoned by contracts, which is why the Indian claim to Indian claims in truth continues to exist. In practice, the Indians have resigned part of the claim to these Indian claims and have retained a part of the country as a reserve country, with this reserve being about 27,000 square kilometres today. The very bad social situation of the Indians is also to be mentioned: The average income of the status Indians is not even half of what a Canadian earns on average, namely 10 500 Canadian dollars compared to an average Canadian income of 24 100 dollars. Over a quarter of the status Indians live on social assistance, unemployment is almost 30 percent, the suicide rate is almost three times as high, not to mention bad training. Now, in Article 35 of the Canadian Constitution, the indigenous rights, including land rights, have been recognised in principle, and the Canadian Supreme Court in 1997 – Mr Lunacek has already pointed out – has recognised the "original title" as a collective property right of indigenous groups. Nevertheless, the Canadian Government has not yet addressed it. In my opinion, therefore, the present proposal would have been a moderate request, which is by no means a interference in the internal affairs of Canada, but that, in talks with Canada, one should strive for a mediating position to move the Canadian government towards cooperation and a distraction towards its own indigenous population. No more and no less in this proposal! Unfortunately, they will reject this request, which is not a good sign of Austrian human rights policy. - Thank you.
